#45. Myanmar: Is the US undermining the EU's diplomatic blockade?

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With the US reopening talks with Myanmar's military-backed government, and several Southeast Asian countries restarting diplomatic contacts, the EU is facing uncomfortable questions about its own boycott of the junta.

The US government has begun a quiet reproachment with Myanmar's military-backed government, holding two rounds of talks since September with State Department representative Michael Vance. American diplomats reportedly meet Myanmar officials in Naypyitaw and then in Tokyo earlier this month.

Washington wants to tackle online scam centers, secure the release of imprisoned American businessman Adam Castillo, and potentially gain access to Myanmar's rare-earth resources. The market for the valuable minerals is currently controlled by the US rival China.

Meanwhile, Southeast Asian governments are also increasingly questioning their policy of isolating Myanmar's military leadership. Multiple countries have already renewed direct diplomatic contacts.

The EU, however, continues to reject the legitimacy of the military junta in the war-torn country. The European bloc has also remained unmoved by the 2025/2026 election, which ended with military chief Min Aung Hlaing becoming the president. The military retains control over the political system.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

The EU responded to Myanmar's February 2021 military coup by suspending direct financial assistance to the government, imposing targeted sanctions and sharply reducing diplomatic engagement with the military authorities.

Its sanctions currently cover 105 individuals and 22 entities connected to the military. In April, the EU extended these measures for another 12 months.

At the same time, Brussels is providing humanitarian assistance to displaced communities and Rohingya refugees and is supporting civil society organizations.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

The EU also stopped short of withdrawing Myanmar's preferential trade access because of concerns about the consequences for workers, particularly the mostly young women employed in the country's garment industry. Last year, bilateral goods trade was worth about €4 billion ($4.46 billion), up from €3.4 billion in 2019.

The civil war triggered by the 2021 coup has killed an estimated 100,000 people. Rebel groups still control or contest large swathes of Myanmar, and over 4 million people have been internally displaced, according to UN data.

The EU "continues to uphold its policy of non-recognition of the current military regime," in view of "the ongoing violence and the attack against its own population," an EU spokesman told DW.

At the same time, the bloc "is closely monitoring developments in the discussions and positions of the United States, Japan, ASEAN countries and other major actors with representatives of Myanmar's military regime."

"It also supports ASEAN efforts, in particular the Five-Point Consensus, which has yet to be fully implemented," the spokesperson added, referring to the peace deal proposed by the Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) in 2021.

In the aftermath of the coup, ASEAN took the unprecedented decision to exclude Myanmar's military leaders from its highest-level meetings after the junta failed to implement the regional peace plan.

However, ASEAN's position is showing signs of change. Some member states, most notably Myanmar's neighbor Thailand, signal growing frustration with the lack of progress towards ending Myanmar's civil war.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Last week, Thai Foreign Minister Sihasak Phuangketkeow welcomed Washington's renewed engagement and suggested that ASEAN members would hold substantive discussions about Myanmar's future at their summit in Manila next month.

Myanmar's authorities are also seeking to break their diplomatic isolation. In September, Myanmar's leader Hlaing gave an interview to Time magazine, his first with a major Western publication since the coup, as part of an apparent effort to rebuild relations with Western governments.

On October 22, Burma Campaign UK, a London-based human rights organization, accused the British government of making a "significant shift in approach" after the British Foreign Office hosted Win Zeyar Tun, the charge d'affaires of Myanmar's military-controlled embassy in London, at an ASEAN diplomatic gathering days earlier.

According to an article published by Intelligence Online this month, the regime's charge d'affaires in Brussels, Khin Thidar Ay, has also sought contacts with European politicians, including Czech MEP Ondrej Dostal, a member of the European Parliament's delegation for Southeast Asia.

Kristina Kironska, of Palacky University Olomouc in the Czech Republic and an expert on the EU's policy on Myanmar, told DW that the EU has little reason to follow Washington's apparent shift towards engagement with the Myanmar junta.

The US strategy "appears to be pursuing specific interests" such as rare earth mining and cybercrime, she said.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

However, the most important rare earth mining operations are no longer controlled by the military, but by rebels such as the Kachin Independence Organization in Kachin State. "It remains to be seen what the US can achieve through negotiations with the junta," she said.

The EU has long positioned itself as a defender of democracy and human rights in Myanmar, "so abandoning this now because the US is exploring a new, perhaps more transactional approach would undermine the EU's credibility," Kironska added.

"At the same time, isolation has achieved relatively little. The question is therefore not whether the EU should engage, but rather with whom and under what conditions."

The EU is forced to consider risks to its own influence. If Washington, ASEAN governments and other regional powers increase their engagement with Myanmar's authorities, the EU risks becoming increasingly marginal in discussions about the country's political future.

Yet greater engagement with the military could undermine Brussels' relations with Myanmar's democratic opposition and ethnic resistance organizations, many of which control substantial territory.

"The key question for Brussels is whether it can remain a credible defender of democratic change while ensuring that it still has a meaningful voice in the negotiations that will determine Myanmar's future," Sabe Soe, director of the NGO Burma Centre Prague, told DW.

One possibility is for Brussels to maintain its sanctions against military leaders and their businesses while expanding diplomacy through ASEAN, the UN and other regional partners. The EU could also tie any easing of restrictions to humanitarian access, improvements in political freedoms and progress towards peace.

Soe also suggested that the EU could pursue economic engagement in areas administered by revolutionary and ethnic resistance forces, offering an alternative to engagement centred on the military authorities in Naypyidaw.

This approach would allow the EU to remain involved in Myanmar's future without necessarily recognizing its military-backed government, she said.

For now, Brussels seems to have little appetite for a fundamental policy rethink. But this could change as more governments reopen their doors to Myanmar's generals.

#10. Flavio Bolsonaro flees Brazil for US amid investigation — reports

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Flavio Bolsonaro is reportedly on the way to the US, according to reports citing people close to the senator. Bolsonaro's passports were canceled earlier Tuesday.

Brazilian Senator Flavio Bolsonaro has left the country for the United States after he was defeated in Sunday's presidential election, Folha de S.Paulo and Reuters reported, citing sources close to the senator.

The Supreme Court had asked Flavio Bolsonaro, the eldest son of former President Jair Bolsonaro, to surrender his passports to stop him from leaving the country amid investigations into the senator.

It was reported that Bolsonaro had refused to comply with the order, although other reports contradicted this.

The AFP news agency cited a police official as saying that Bolsonaro's passports had been cancelled and there was no official record of him leaving the country.

Brazilian newspaper Folha de S.Paulo reported that Bolsonaro's plane was scheduled to land in either Miami or Dallas.

The news came after Brazilians spent the day on social media asking where the former presidential candidate was.

Argentine news outlet Infobae reported earlier today that Bolsonaro had crossed into Argentina to board a plane to the US.

A spokesperson for Argentina's Ministry of Security denied the reports that the conservative Brazilian senator had entered the country.

Brazilian media reports earlier said that the federal police were still searching for him.

Bolsonaro lost Brazil's presidential election runoff on Sunday after an incredibly tight voting count through the eveninguntil his challenger, incumbent President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva, surged ahead.

Bolsonaro had initially said he would seek to annul the election results, citing alleged irregularities.But Bolsonaro's Liberal Party on Tuesday acknowledged the results.

In a video on Instagram on Monday, Bolsonaro said he had been served with an order to hand over his passports so he couldn't leave the country.

He had said he was planning to hand over his passports but reports on Tuesday said the deadline had passed leading to their cancellation.

The senator has been accused of laundering money through a sports betting company, money that was deducted from government pensions to senior citizens.

A second investigation is looking into Bolsonaro's connections to a disgraced banker from whom he allegedly accepted millions of dollars to finance the biopic about his father, titled "Dark Horse."

Bolsonaro loses his immunity from prosecution once his Senate term comes to end in February 2027. He has denied wrongdoing in both cases.

Bolsonaro said that he had received the Supreme Court order outside the home where his father, Jair Bolsonaro, citing alleged irregularities. following his 2022 election loss, also to Lula.

He said the order was issued by Supreme Court Justice Alexandre de Moraes, the same judge who sentenced his father to prison.

He also pointed to comments made by his brother, Eduardo Bolsonaro, who said on a podcast last week that his sibling should leave the country if he lost the election.

Eduardo, who lives in the US, was convicted in absentia in June of illegally lobbying the Trump administration. He was sentenced to four years in prison and a warrant was issued for his arrest.

#5618. Dramatic Changes Are Unlikely in Israel, Even if Netanyahu Is Ousted

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

This issue is preventing our website from loading properly. Please review the following troubleshooting tips or contact us at [email protected].

Analysis: Dramatic Changes Are Unlikely in Israel, Even if Netanyahu Is Ousted

Create an FP account to save articles to read later.

Downloadable PDFs are a benefit of an FP subscription.

Contact us at [email protected] to learn about upgrade options, unlocking the ability to gift this article.

Get audio access with any FP subscription.

Long before Israel’s 2023 war in Gaza, the Israeli military formulated an offensive strategy known as the Dahiya Doctrine. The idea was to inflict such massive force against an enemy stronghold, including broad destruction of civilian and economic infrastructure, that the adversary would be deterred from tangling with Israel ever again. The doctrine was implemented in Israel’s 2006 war with Hezbollah in Lebanon—Dahiya is a southern suburb of Beirut and a stronghold of the group. It was coined by Gadi Eisenkot, who served at the time as the head of the military’s operations directorate.

I am reminded of this episode because Israel just had its long-awaited election, and early returns suggest Eisenkot might possibly be the country’s next prime minister. According to Israeli television’s Channel 12 exit poll, Eisenkot’s centrist “change bloc” is projected to control 52 seats in the 120-member parliament, compared to 50 seats for Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu’s right-wing bloc and 18 for the Arab-led parties. The results are preliminary—it will take days to count all the ballots and weeks or more before a governing coalition comes together. Eisenkot’s path to power is hardly assured.

Long before Israel’s 2023 war in Gaza, the Israeli military formulated an offensive strategy known as the Dahiya Doctrine. The idea was to inflict such massive force against an enemy stronghold, including broad destruction of civilian and economic infrastructure, that the adversary would be deterred from tangling with Israel ever again. The doctrine was implemented in Israel’s 2006 war with Hezbollah in Lebanon—Dahiya is a southern suburb of Beirut and a stronghold of the group. It was coined by Gadi Eisenkot, who served at the time as the head of the military’s operations directorate.

I am reminded of this episode because Israel just had its long-awaited election, and early returns suggest Eisenkot might possibly be the country’s next prime minister. According to Israeli television’s Channel 12 exit poll, Eisenkot’s centrist “change bloc” is projected to control 52 seats in the 120-member parliament, compared to 50 seats for Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu’s right-wing bloc and 18 for the Arab-led parties. The results are preliminary—it will take days to count all the ballots and weeks or more before a governing coalition comes together. Eisenkot’s path to power is hardly assured.

If the trend does hold and Netanyahu is ousted, this will please many people in Washington who want to see substantial changes in Israeli policy. But here’s something they should keep in mind. On the issues that journalists, analysts, and some members of Congress care most about—the Gaza Strip, the West Bank, and a two-state solution—Eisenkot’s change bloc does not differ all that much from the current coalition. On most of these issues, Netanyahu has been firmly within Israel’s mainstream, just like his opponents.

Much of Washington is afflicted with Bibi Derangement Syndrome. They have a hard time discerning between what is actually the fault of the prime minister and facts about Israeli politics that observers happen to not like. This syndrome underlines the inability of both its passionate supporters and its equally passionate critics to treat Israel as a normal country whose leaders confront internal political constraints and incentives. I don’t mean to let Netanyahu off the hook. He has rubbed a fair number of people the wrong way and played partisan politics in Washington. Former U.S. Secretary of State James Baker banned him from the State Department when Netanyahu said U.S. policy in the region was based on “lies and distortions.” President Bill Clinton, after meeting Netanyahu for the first time in 1996, asked: “Who the fuck does he think he is? Who’s the fucking superpower here?”

President Barack Obama and Netanyahu clearly could not stand to be in the same room. Jared Kushner relays in his book about the first Trump administration that his father-in-law always expected Netanyahu to undercut him. And President Joe Biden, who thought he knew Israel better than everyone, clearly found dealing with the Israeli leader to be trying. And what Americans who dislike him say about Netanyahu is mild in comparison to his Israeli opponents. And yet, personal tics aside, the prime minister clearly reflects a broader band of the Israeli electorate than many in the policy world want to believe.

On Sept. 26, Eisenkot and other leaders of the change bloc—Naftali Bennett, Yair Lapid, Avigdor Lieberman, and Yair Golan—released a document detailing their priorities should they form a government. In it, they announced a government of repair or reconstruction that would prioritize the establishment of a state commission of inquiry into the failures surrounding the Hamas attacks on Oct. 7, 2023, promised to pass term limits for the prime minister, draft a constitution, and oppose Haredi exemption from military service.

There was nothing in the document about the Gaza Strip; nothing about the E-1 settlement, which the current government has begun the process of building decades after its approval and which it is feared would make it even harder for Palestinians to travel between the northern and southern West Bank. And, crucially, there was nothing about a two-state solution. One might assume these topics were left out so that the platform could appeal to the widest possible group of Israelis—and that there will be some flexibility once a new government is in place. But after spending almost two weeks in Israel, talking to political figures, analysts, and just everyday Israelis, I am not so sure that’s the case.

On a warm Sunday evening, about 30 Israelis and I gathered in the living room of a modest home in central Israel to listen to two members of Beyachad, the joint list of Bennett’s “Bennett 2026” party and Yesh Atid under Lapid’s leadership. Both candidates emphasized the need for political and social reform in an Israel they see as battered and abused by almost two decades of governments suborned to Netanyahu’s parochial and personal interests at the expense of the rest of society. They were just as critical on foreign policy, blasting Netanyahu for Israel’s low international standing and not making the country’s case more forcefully and effectively in important national capitals and international fora like the United Nations. It was at this moment when the conversation took a turn many in Washington would not expect: Both candidates assailed the government for bungling Gaza, Lebanon, and Iran—making the case that for all of the effort Israel had expended across these battle zones, all three remained a threat. This was not a call for diplomatic solutions to the problems of Iran and its proxies, but a more effective military strategy. This position was hardly controversial among people who are wary of American and European demands for agreements that do little more than allow Israel’s enemies to live and fight another day.

They also stressed their opposition to a Palestinian state. They offered that after Oct. 7, the Palestinians could expect nothing more from a new Israeli government than autonomy in Area A (of the West Bank)—an artifact of the Oslo era in which the Palestinian Authority manages the affairs of the population. When one member of the small audience asked them about the situation in the West Bank, using the term “West Bank,” Bennett’s representative made sure he emphasized “Judea and Samaria,” the preferred terminology among the settlers for the West Bank that has become normalized among many Israelis. And when asked about their alliance with Yair Golan, whose Democrats party is considered left of center, both candidates reiterated their opposition to Palestinian statehood. Most Israelis agree with this position, according to polls.

The concern about Golan among at least one person attending the Beyachad event demonstrates just how opposed Israelis are to a Palestinian state. Golan’s position is hardly dovish. He opposes settlement expansion because he fears it would lead inevitably to annexation and a binational state. Instead, Golan believes that over the long term, a two-state solution is best for Israeli security, but because of Oct. 7, Golan supports “civil separation” and an indefinite security presence in the West Bank until a responsible Palestinian leadership can take over. I dare someone to define “long term” and “responsible” in this context. And even if you could get to this semantic promised land, Golan, like almost all Israeli politicians, believes Israel must hold on to the Jordan Valley, further truncating a Palestinian state.

It’s not that nothing would change under a new Israeli government led by the change bloc. Israel would work quickly to establish law and order in the West Bank, where settler violence against Palestinians has surged lately. Of the multiple pressures facing Israel, it is the easiest issue to deal with, but it is not easy. There will be a lot of politics around a crackdown, especially since this issue of settler violence is more complicated than the press, analysts, and members of Congress portray. Ben-Gvir could be out of power, but he is not going away, especially since he commands a loyal following among Israel’s middle and lower classes. He is among a strain on the Israeli right who do not care about sanctions or Israel’s standing in the West.

It is possible that Israel will have a new prime minister soon. And while Israel’s tone would likely be less pugnacious under a Prime Minister Eisenkot or Bennett, all the issues that separate the United States and Israel will remain. It is hard to see how a new government papers over the differences. It would be better to respectfully disagree and let the U.S.-Israel relationship change. A little bit of space would be good for everyone.

Steven A. Cook is a columnist at Foreign Policy and the Eni Enrico Mattei senior fellow for Middle East and Africa studies at the Council on Foreign Relations. His latest book is The End of Ambition: America’s Past, Present, and Future in the Middle East. X: @stevenacook

#3348. If You're Worried Inflation Will Wreck Your Retirement, Here Are 3 Must-Dos

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Inflation can be a serious threat to your retirement plans. And while inflation doesn't always hit people smack in the face like it does today, it can still be a problem.

Even when prices rise by relatively modest amounts, the cumulative effect over 20 or 30 years can significantly reduce the purchasing power of your savings. Expenses that seem manageable at the start of retirement, from groceries to utilities to healthcare, could consume a much larger share of your retirement income in the future.

The good news is that you don't have to accept inflation as an unavoidable threat to your retirement. Here are three important moves that could give you a leg up on inflation.

Your Social Security benefits might play a meaningful role in your retirement income. Once you turn 62, you can begin collecting benefits at any time. If you wait for your full retirement age to arrive, you'll get your monthly benefits without a reduction.

However, if you delay your Social Security claim past full retirement age, your benefits are eligible for an 8% boost for each year you wait, up to age 70. With a full retirement age of 67, which is the case if you were born in 1960 or later, you can potentially score a 24% increase to your monthly checks.

A larger monthly benefit can be particularly valuable when inflation starts to soar. Social Security benefits receive annual cost-of-living adjustments (COLAs) that are designed to help those payments keep pace with inflation. Starting with a larger monthly benefit can mean receiving larger dollar increases over time.

Of course, delaying isn't right for everyone. Your health, life expectancy, and other factors need to be considered in your Social Security claiming decision. But if you're able to cover your expenses from retirement savings or other income sources while you wait, delaying Social Security until age 70 could make it easier to manage inflation throughout your senior years.

Keeping all of your retirement savings in cash or low-yielding investments might feel like the safest move. But it could expose you to substantial purchasing-power risk over time. If inflation averages 3% annually, but your savings only grow 2% annually, you're going to fall behind.

Stocks have historically provided long-term growth that outpaces inflation. Although the stock market can be volatile, a diversified portfolio of high-quality companies or index funds could help your savings keep up without exposing you to undue risk -- especially if you limit stocks to a portion of your portfolio only.

The right percentage and investment mix depends on your risk tolerance and income needs. For some people, a 65% stock allocation might work. For others, 50% may be optimal. Either way, the goal is to balance growth potential with stability so that your portfolio can support near-term and long-term withdrawals.

Even with a strategic portfolio and a boosted monthly Social Security benefit, inflation can strain your finances if your spending plan is too rigid. It's important to be flexible when costs skyrocket because a few months of cutting back could help your savings last longer.

Start by separating essential expenses such as housing, food, utilities, and healthcare from discretionary costs such as travel and entertainment. That distinction could help you identify where to adjust your spending if prices rise faster than expected or your investments underperform.

Also revisit your budget regularly. If markets decline or essential expenses surge, temporarily reducing nonessential purchases could help preserve your savings.

Inflation has the potential to erode your buying power over time. But that doesn't mean you have to sit back and let it. By delaying Social Security, choosing the right investments, and being willing to adjust your spending as needed, you can set yourself up to thrive even as costs increase throughout your retirement.

#4316. Tesla's Cybercab Fixes A Big Repairability Problem With Its Structural Batteries

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

Tesla helped pioneer a shift toward structural battery packs that eliminate traditional modules to save weight and squeeze more energy into a smaller space. But with its latest vehicle, America's EV leader is moving in the other direction in the name of repairability.

Instead of embedding hundreds of in-house 4680 battery cells into a single, tightly integrated assembly, as Tesla did with the structural battery packs found in some Model Ys and the Cybertruck, the Cybercab contains four separate modules of those cells. That means a faulty module can be swapped out without replacing the entire battery pack. Tesla executives explained the decision in a video on YouTube channel of the engineering firm Munro & Associates.

"It's good to be self-critical," said Robert Sumpf, the director of mechanical design engineering for Tesla's battery modules and packs. "I'm going to give our Model Y structural battery a grade D," he added, noting that it was an "awesome product" to the customer but was hard to build.

Tesla 4680 battery cells assembled in a structural pack, without the potting shown.

The other issue with the structural approach is serviceability, he said. Since all the cells are bound together in the pack using a hard potting material, it's practically impossible to repair the battery if one cell is defective, for example. Indeed, after tearing down a Model Y with a structural pack a few years back, Munro & Associates concluded that its battery had "zero repairability."

There are advantages to getting rid of conventional module structures (the building blocks of a battery pack) and making the battery itself structural to the vehicle, in that the design lets manufacturers stuff more cells into a given area and save some material costs. It's why automakers including BMW, Porsche, and Ford are now designing cars with cells that go directly into packs or even directly into the vehicle's body structure.

Tesla's top brass, though, didn't think the serviceability tradeoff was worth it in the Cybercab. Sumpf described the Cybercab's design as "semi-structural." "You're going to get maybe 10% more volume for cells in a structural pack when you delete the structure," Sumpf said. "You're going to save a little cost as a result, but the issue is the service cost."

In general, studies show that EV batteries rarely fail. Degradation is typically predictable and manageable over the lifetime of a consumer EV. However, the Cybercab is supposed to hold up hundreds of thousands of miles of round-the-clock abuse as a robotaxi. That may help explain the decision to switch things up battery-wise. Sure, a structural, module-free pack may help eke out a few extra miles of range, which is great for selling EVs to regular people. But for a fleet-based business, total cost of ownership (TCO) is everything.

"When you think of TCO as we've talked about, replacing an entire pack before half a million miles is a killer on cost," Eric Earley, the Cybercab's chief engineer, told the Munro Live YouTube channel. "So we have to think of [remanufacturing], we have to think of pack serviceability, we have to think of all those things that actually get us to our cost target for the product."

#5041. Bitcoin Could Follow 2018 and 2022 Playbook as U.S. Midterms Near Before Rally, Analyst Warns

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin (BTC) could face further losses ahead of the November 3 U.S. midterm elections before recovering in 2027, as analysts compare it to previous election cycles.

In a Tuesday post on X, CryptoQuant analyst Satoureireal cited data from blockchain analysis firm Alphractal showing that Bitcoin suffered significant corrections around the 2018 and 2022 midterms before recovering the following year. BTC bottomed in late 2018 before rebounding in 2019, while the 2022 bear market gave way to a recovery in 2023.

With the next midterms approaching, Satoureireal questioned whether the same pattern could emerge in 2026, potentially setting the stage for a recovery in 2027.

In a September 13 post, the analyst also highlighted recurring volatility around U.S. midterms, noting that Bitcoin has sometimes rallied before an election and sold off afterward, while declines have emerged later in other cycles.

“During the previous two midterm election cycles in 2018 and 2022, Bitcoin experienced significant corrections before forming a bottom and entering a recovery phase in the following year,” Satoureireal stated.

Notably, this distinction matters because the historical relationship does not prove that elections cause Bitcoin sell-offs. Market liquidity, monetary policy, investor positioning, and broader economic conditions can also influence price movements.

Elsewhere, Technical analyst Frank Cappelleri has also identified similarities between Bitcoin’s current chart and the price structure that preceded its previous major recovery.

Speaking to CNBC recently, the CappThesis founder compared Bitcoin’s roughly 54% decline during the current cycle with the nearly 70% drawdown recorded between 2022 and 2023.

“This also looks like a bullish pattern to me,” Cappelleri said, pointing to how the earlier decline eventually gave way to a strong recovery as market momentum returned.

However, he stopped short of predicting another rally as large as Bitcoin’s previous advance, which approached 400%.

“I’m not calling for that, but I am calling for a very similar foundation to be leveraged and potentially get back to new highs again,” he said.

His analysis suggests Bitcoin could establish a base for another advance, potentially reaching new highs in early 2027.

That said, not all historical comparisons support an immediate bullish outlook.

Recently, popular analyst Ali Charts highlighted Bitcoin’s declines following the previous four U.S. midterm elections.

According to his analysis, Bitcoin fell 72% after the 2010 midterms, 65% after the 2014 elections, 52% after the 2018 vote and 27% following the 2022 midterms.

In a thread on X, the analyst identified $73,000 as a level to watch if the historical pattern repeats.

Meanwhile, on-chain data point to changes in investor behavior that could influence Bitcoin’s next move.

Glassnode reported that profit-taking by short-term Bitcoin holders has eased after contributing to selling pressure in recent weeks.

The analytics firm said the share of exchange inflows from short-term holders sitting on profits reached a one-year high before returning to its average level over the past year.

Santiment also reported that wallets holding between 10 and 10,000 BTC accumulated approximately 88,987 BTC in one month from September 19. Their combined holdings exceeded 13.69 million BTC, the highest level since April, according to the analytics firm.

The firm noted that continued accumulation could support market confidence despite price volatility.

“Bitcoin still drives much of crypto’s direction, so persistent accumulation from its largest influential holders can support not only BTC, but confidence across the market.” The firm noted.

At press time, Bitcoin was trading at $80,341, up 1.02% in the past 24 hours.

As shoot days fall, the city is making civic properties more accessible to crews and offering free microshoot permits.

As filming levels in the city continue their downward momentum, the Los Angeles City Council passed two measures in a meeting on Wednesday to attempt to stem the bleeding.

The measures, both introduced by City Councilmember Adrin Nazarian, create a free microshoot permit for ultra-small productions and open up city-owned parking lots, yards, civic properties and staging areas to productions for just $1 a day.

The microshoot motion will provide small projects with 10 or fewer production personnel with permits to film on-location in L.A. without a fee, making filmmaking less onerous and expensive for student filmmakers, documentary crews and ultra-low-budget projects in the city. Related Stories Business Los Angeles Film and TV Shoot Days Keep Plunging As Local Officials Scramble for a Turnaround Plan Business New York Poised to Overtake California as Top State for Movie and TV Production

“Today marks a major milestone for the Keep Hollywood Home campaign,” Nazarian said in a statement. “With these latest actions, all Phase 1 motions have now been approved by the City. This progress demonstrates the impact of the Ad Hoc Committee and our commitment to keep up the pressure for real reforms.”

The development arrives less than a week after city film office FilmLA found that shoot days for film and television in the city continued to fall from June to September in spite of local and state efforts to encourage more production to return to the city. L.A.’s shoot days dropped nearly five percent compared to the same period in 2025 and 11 percent from the previous quarter.

In 2025, California more than doubled the funding available for its film and television tax credit program, from a previous $330 million a year to $750 million. On a more local level, Mayor Karen Bass issued an executive order in 2025 to streamline the city’s permitting process. Meanwhile, seven initial motions from Nazarian to improve filming practices in L.A. passed the full Council earlier this year.

#3355. A $20 AGNC Share Bought at Its 2008 IPO Has Paid $51 in Dividends. The Stock Trades Near $9.

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

At first glance, AGNC (AGNC +1.02%) might seem like a terrible long-term investment. The mortgage real estate investment trust (mREIT) went public at $20 per share on May 15, 2008, but now trades at less than $9 per share.

But since AGNC's market debut, it's paid out $51.16 per share in dividends. If you had reinvested those dividends, you'd be sitting on a total return of roughly 520%. Let's see why there's such a gap between its stock price performance and dividend-driven gains -- and if it's still a good long-term play for income-oriented investors.

As an mREIT, AGNC invests in mortgages and mortgage-backed securities to collect interest. To insulate it from another credit crunch, it mainly invests in agency-backed MBS. It must distribute at least 90% of its taxable income as dividends to maintain a lower tax rate.

AGNC borrows at short-term rates (through "repo" transactions that enable it to sell its MBS to counterparties and buy them back at a set price plus interest at a future date) to fund its purchases of longer-term MBS. For AGNC to keep growing, short-term rates need to be lower than long-term rates. The mortgage market also needs to remain healthy.

Since AGNC distributes most of its profits to its investors, it must continually issue new shares to fund its MBS purchases. That's why its total number of shares skyrocketed from 15 million at the time of its IPO to approximately 1.26 billion shares today. That constant dilution practically guarantees it will underperform the S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.60%) over the long term.

AGNC pays a massive forward dividend yield of 16.4%. That might seem like a bright red flag, but it's sustainable as long as its net interest spread (the gap between the average yield it earns on its MBS and the costs of funding those purchases) stays positive.

That figure has hovered around 2% over the past year, and its projected 2026 EPS of $1.56 will easily cover its forward dividend rate of $1.44 per share. At $9, it trades at less than six times this year's earnings -- so it certainly looks like an undervalued income stock.

However, AGNC isn't necessarily the best dividend play for long-term investors. If you had simply invested in an S&P 500 index fund when AGNC went public and reinvested your dividends, you'd be sitting on a bigger total return of 669%. So while AGNC's dividend payments offset its declining stock price, it still couldn't outperform the market over the long term.

#3615. ‘Manhood’ Review: Penis Enlargement Documentary Curves Left of Center

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Editor’s Note: This review was originally published at SXSW 2026. World of Wonder releases “Manhood” in select theaters starting Wednesday, October 28.

What if you could up your penis size a notch or two with a few spritzes of a needle? OK, it’s not that simple, but the PhaloFILL procedure highlighted by filmmaker Daniel Lombroso in his latest documentary, “Manhood,” aims to make penile enlargement procedures about as easy (and commonplace) as Botox.

Dallas entrepreneur Bill Moore, who is quite proudly Botoxed and fillered-up himself, patented the minimally invasive procedure (don’t call it plastic surgery!), promising to make insecure men feel whole again. Or at least a bit thicker, anyway. Lombroso’s skillfully constructed film is funny in the best and worst ways: funny because it’s in on the joke but “funny” because of how the majority of the audience might feel embarrassed just by watching it. The director understands the humor inherent to this situation, but he also understands the sad parts, the well of male insecurity it’s all coming from — locating that especially in an OnlyFans camboy who comes to Moore after a catastrophic procedure at a rival clinic has left him with a dick full of nodules. Related Stories Will ‘You Can See Everything’ Catch ‘Fahrenheit 9/11’ to Be the Highest-Grossing Documentary Ever? ‘The Mosquito Bowl’ Review: ‘Friday Night Lights’ Meets ‘Five Came Back’ in Peter Berg’s Formulaic Netflix Movie About a World War II Football Game

Penis size is a major psychic hang-up for men across both the straight and gay communities; as a gay man, I can attest to these particular pressures within a community where both partners have penises, and a community that already primatizes physical attributes over the inner self. Moore’s practice, as “Manhood” illustrates, has turned small genitalia (or even those not especially small, even though the body-dysmorphic men they’re attached to insist they are because of society, etc., etc.) into a growing business while seeking to universalize the dick-size complex. “Every man wants a bigger penis,” the bio-hacking mogul says at one point. Do they? Using hyaluronic acid — which is the same stuff I put on my face every morning — he’s ready to give you the big penis of your dreams, and an even bigger bill.

One of Moore’s clients, endearingly self-defeating Arizona-resident Ruben, is a father of five who is under serious financial strain due to gambling addiction and also perhaps a penile enhancement addiction. He’s a repeat customer of Moore’s, having spent tens of thousands on multiple procedures that — this is important — have not increased the length of his penis but certainly the girth several sizes over. Penis lengthening is not something any doctor wishing to avoid malpractice has successfully figured out, despite grotesque procedures in ancient times, from mechanical lengthening to snake bites, that purported to do so.

Meanwhile, though Ruben’s wife was plenty happy with his God-given girth, her husband (who has children with multiple women) keeps going back to Dr. Moore for yet another below-the-belt tune-up. “Manhood” is loud and clear in its uncensored depiction of these injections into men’s members as they drop their drawers and splay prostrate on the operating table. Don’t forget the gasp-inducing images of dicks mangled by one Dr. Loria in New York City, who has proudly refused malpractice insurance but allegedly left a number of men deformed after pumping them up with chunky-looking filler. Lombroso applies the same close-up care as he did in his 2022 documentary “White Noise,” unflinchingly gazing into the rise of the alt-right movement and neo-Nazis in America. “Manhood” isn’t reinventing the form — even as the men it follows are reinventing theirs — but it’s super charming in its straightforward aims at tackling taboo material.

Contrasting Ruben’s heterosexual working-class charm, meek-mannered David Smith, a former OnlyFans camboy turned Florida nursing student, is one such emotional and physical casualty of Dr. Loria’s alleged malpractice. (Loria, by the way, is an unapologetic talking head in this documentary, too.) Already coming from a devoutly conservative family that rejected his homosexuality, the tight-bodied, petite David felt insecure about his dick size while becoming a fast-rising star on OnlyFans. He’s been left with what Dr. Moore describes as “the penis of an alien.” Moore not only offers David the promise to fix his penis, he also invites him into his bed to be the apparent third in Moore’s relationship with his partner. The three are seen sharing a hotel bed while traveling to see a specialist who will need to perform drastic surgery on David’s member, and it’s not clear what Lambroso, or his camera, thinks of this arrangement, which is why this decidedly non-invasive, nonjudgmental documentary works so well.

“I can fill your penis with filler, but I cannot fill the hole in your heart,” Moore tells his patient/lover David. The patient-doctor relationship here is certainly ethically questionable, but at what point are ethics just sort of tossed out the window when we’re talking dicks? Still, Lombroso takes his subject and his subjects seriously, and treats them with respect. David, marveling near-tears at the results of his reconstructive surgery, makes for a moving moment — who knew a documentary about penile enhancement could touch a nerve and the heart, too?

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. It wasn't immediately clear if Robinhood's earnings were comparable with LSEG estimates.

Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

#3380. Why Royal Caribbean Stock Climbed Today

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Shares of Royal Caribbean (RCL +5.46%) rallied on Tuesday after the cruise ship operator reported higher-than-expected profits and boosted its full-year earnings forecast.

Royal Caribbean's revenue rose 9% year over year to $5.6 billion in the third quarter.

"Our strategy to turn the vacation of a lifetime into a lifetime of vacations is propelling our business by leveraging our leading brands, differentiated experiences, loyalty platform, and technology capabilities to deepen guest relationships, increase vacation frequency, and capture a greater share of the growing global vacation market," CEO Jason Liberty said.

To that end, Royal Caribbean struck a deal to acquire a 50% interest in Sandals and Beaches Resorts in September, giving it access to 18 resorts across the Caribbean, with more under construction.

"By bringing together Sandals and Beaches' hospitality and Caribbean expertise with our global commercial reach, loyalty, and technology capabilities, we see meaningful opportunities to reach new guests, increase vacation frequency, and leverage our combined scale to create additional synergies and value over time," Liberty said.

All told, Royal Caribbean's adjusted net income jumped 12% to $1.8 billion, or $6.57 per share. That exceeded Wall Street's estimates, which had called for per-share profits of $6.38.

These strong results prompted Royal Caribbean to lift its full-year profit target. Management now projects adjusted earnings per share of $17.85 to $17.95, up from a prior forecast of $17.73 to $17.87.

"Demand for our leading vacation experiences remains strong, and while it is still early, 2027 bookings are pacing ahead of historical levels at higher rates," Liberty said.

Joe Tenebruso has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

#5890. Easy (Paid) GHA Discovery Titanium & Platinum Status Match: My Experience

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

GHA Discovery is a hotel loyalty program that flies under the radar, but really shouldn’t. It offers the ability to earn and redeem rewards, and take advantage of elite perks, at hundreds of properties around the globe, including some small, luxury hotel groups (like Capella Hotels & Resorts).

Along those lines, GHA Discovery is currently offering a lucrative paid status match promotion in partnership with statusmatch.com, which may interest some people. This was available earlier in the year, and the offer has just returned. I’d like to go over the details, and also share my experience, as I took advantage of this the last time it was offered.

GHA Discovery is offering a status match to either Titanium or Platinum status. This is an outright match, and status is valid through December 31, 2027 (so there’s no status challenge element to this). This offer is valid for applications through November 30, 2026, though that remains subject to change, and the offer could be pulled earlier, depending on interest.

Matching to GHA Discovery Titanium comes with a fee of $150, but offers a reward of D$75, while matching to GHA Discovery Platinum comes with a fee of $100, but offers a reward of D$50.

For those not familiar, the “D$” refers to Discovery Dollars, and the idea is that each D$ gets you a dollar off an eligible hotel stay. In other words, this match will really only cost you $50-75, assuming you are able to use the D$.

If you go to the application page and click on the FAQs, you can see a matrix that shows how status maps over between programs. GHA Discovery is matching status with airlines, hotels, and cruise lines, and generally upper tier status earns Titanium, while lower tier status earns Platinum. Certain credit cards are also being matched.

I slept on the GHA Discovery program for far too long. I don’t want to say it’s the world’s most exciting loyalty program, but a few things stand out to me:

I decided to submit a GHA Discovery status match request. I appreciate how there’s no challenge requirement to it, how it’s valid for well over a year, and also how many brands are included. For example, I love Capella properties, and plan to stay at more of those while this status is valid, so hopefully that comes in handy.

How did my status match application go? It was super painless, and took maybe one minute. When you go to the page to request a match, you’ll first be asked to enter your first and last name, your email address, and whether you’re trying to match airline status, hotel status, or a credit card.

On the next page, you’ll be asked to enter specifically which program you’re trying to match, what status you have, and what your membership number is.

On the next page, you have to upload proof of your status. This can either come in the form of taking a picture of a physical card, or otherwise taking a photo of your computer screen or tablet, while logged into your account. You can’t edit or crop the screen image.

Next, you’ll be asked to review the application, and pay the fee.

You then receive confirmation of the successful request, noting that it can take two business days for status to be upgraded, and that it can take 10 business days for the bonus D$ to post.

A little under 24 hours after submitting my application, I received an email indicating that my status match was approved, and it included a link to activate my new status.

That link asked me to log into my GHA Discovery account, and then I could immediately confirm that I wanted my status upgraded.

Sure enough, after clicking that button, my status was instantly upgraded.

When it comes to the bonus D$ offered as part of the status match, those ended up posting to my account after six days, well under the promised 10 business days, so that’s good news.

GHA Discovery is offering an easy paid status match promotion for Titanium and Platinum status, with status valid through the end of 2027. While the program has a $100-150 application fee, you receive D$50-D$75, so that helps offset that cost.

If you have any number of GHA stays planned over the next 18+ months, this could seriously be worth considering. It’s an offer I just took advantage of, as I see value here. My status match came through in a little under a day, and then my bonus D$ posted after six days.

Do you plan on taking advantage of this GHA Discovery status match?

I always had GHA as my backup loyalty program. But my experience been so good with their properties that I basically consider it my main, even more because its so easy to keep the status. I wouldnt pay for it by the way, unless you expect instant benefits on your first stay, because its very easy to have it. I hope they keep improving it! Not like Marriot, Hilton, IHG and these guys, that are only going down.

Those complaining about inconsistency are correct but they're also wrong. Aggregating very different chains operating in different markets and targetting different customers cannot produce a consistent outcome. Getting two breakfasts comped at a €56 NH in the Spanish interior is likely to mean that the hotel barely makes the marginal cost of the room being occupied. Upgrades may not always be feasible, or they might just involve the same room with the addition of bathrobes, slippers, and a Nespresso machine. What I've found consistent as a long-term Titanium status holder (in fact I also held a lower-level status tier with NH before GHA was even created) is the willingness of hotel staff to recognise the status and do their best for their guests. I've had profuse apologies for even minor service failures, real money taken off my bill the one time that I did have serious issues with a stay, check out times extended until 18:00 etc. Contrary to some of the bigger chains, GHA hotels seem quite eager to please elite guests.

There are also complimentary status match for the holder of Visa/Mastercard in APAC region (China, SEA, AUS, NZ, etc.) with Visa Infinite/MC World Elite matches to GHA Titanium. So, that's worth taking a look at if you are based in these countries. I matched mine earlier this year and the status immediately posted after they confirmed my card.

I decided to do this speculatively since I have 18 months to recoup the USD 150 for Titanium and half of that is a a cash equivalent, so if I have only one stay in 18 months (unlikely) I will have paid USD 75 to have this additional status available to me, and that's not counting any breakfast or upgrade value on the initial stay. Approval took about 12 hours. I do agree that the North American properties are very limited (almost none in Canada) but I'm intrigued by the access to hotel brands not usually on my radar whose footprint lies outside major North American markets. So I'm treating this as a $75 experiment rather than a guaranteed portal to boundless luxury on every trip.

I looked into this, last month. The elite benefits being applied are inconsistent across brands. Like one sub-brand did not provide complimentary breakfast for the highest status. Other that did not provide upgrades. Same with FHR bookings...certain subbrands do not recognize status for FHR bookings. But a handful of brands do. The word is they are trying to get better and more consistent. But they do understand they are not quiet there as yet. I might revisit this in a few years. For now, I pass.

#3571. Kevin Bleyer, Emmy-Winning ‘Daily Show’ Writer, Dies at 54 in Car Crash

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

He also worked on shows hosted by Bill Maher and Dennis Miller, authored books and penned speeches for President Obama.

Kevin Bleyer, who won four writing Emmys for his work on The Daily Show, died Sunday in a car crash, his family announced. He was 54.

Bleyer was driving on a rural road in Millrift, Pennslyvania, where he had a home, when he was killed, his publicist told The Hollywood Reporter. No one else was hurt.

Bleyer worked on the ABC late-night show Politically Incorrect, hosted by Bill Maher, from 2000 until its 2002 cancelation and wrote for Dennis Miller’s CNBC show during its lone 2004-05 season before he landed on Jon Stewart’s Daily Show at Comedy Central in 2005. Related Stories TV Susan Saint James, 'Name of the Game,' 'McMillan & Wife' and 'Kate & Allie' Star, Dies at 80 Movies Hubert de la Bouillerie, 'Woodstock' and 'Witches of Eastwick' Film Editor, Dies at 75

He spent nine years as a staff writer on the program through 2013 while also writing for the 2006 and 2008 Oscars that Stewart hosted. He and others shared Emmys for their work in 2006, 2009, 2011 and 2012 and were nominated four other times.

The Peabody and WGA award winner also served as a writer and producer on NBC’s Today show and as a supervising producer for MSNBC’s Ronan Farrow Daily and wrote a weekly column for The Daily Beast.

For scripted television, he wrote for the Disney+ series The Right Stuff — an adaptation of the Tom Wolfe book — Fox’s Sleepy Hollow and Bravo’s Significant Others and created and executive produced pilots for Disney Television Animation, Comedy Central, Nickelodeon and Showtime.

Born in Bethesda, Maryland, Bleyer was raised in Seattle and attended Stanford University. He called New York City his home for the past 20 years.

He began his career as a dramaturg and assistant to Anna Deavere Smith at New York’s Public Theater and Broadway’s Cort Theater before moving to London, where he worked for journalist David Brancaccio at London Bureau of Public Radio International’s business affairs program Marketplace.

He was a co-author with Stewart and others of 2010’s Earth: The Book and the author of another best-seller, 2012’s Me the People: One Man’s Selfless Quest to Rewrite the Constitution of the United States of America.

More recently, he was the co-author, with former U.S. ambassador to the United Nations and New Mexico Gov. Bill Richardson, of the 2013 book How to Sweet-Talk a Shark, which recounted among other adventures their humanitarian mission to North Korea to negotiate the release of hostage Kenneth Bae.

As a speechwriter, Bleyer wrote for President Obama, contributing to all of his addresses at the annual White House Correspondents’ Association dinners. He also served for a time as the deputy chief speechwriter for New York Mayor Bill de Blasio.

In 2013, Kevin lived in Kyrgyzstan, where he executive produced Studio 7, the first political satire news show in Central Asia. Upon returning, he co-founded Pilot Media Initiatives, which launches civics-minded media programs in democratically challenged countries. And he produced and helped launch scripted, unscripted and political satire television shows in Macedonia, Nigeria, Moldova, Kenya, Jordan and Estonia in the past decade.

“Kevin Bleyer was a writer’s writer. From his many years on The Daily Show to writing Obama’s WHCD speeches to his many books and his work on numerous scripted dramas, Kevin could literally do it all,” actor and screenwriter Danny Strong said in a statement. “His immense talent was only outmatched by his kind soul. I am in complete shock that he is gone, and my sadness is overwhelming. I already miss him dearly.”

Survivors include his wife, Nella; his father, Archie; his brother, Keith; his sister-in-law, Kelly; his nephew, Evan; and his niece, Lauren.

“Kevin spent his life finding the perfect words to make people laugh, think and see the world a little differently,” his wife said in a statement. “I was lucky enough to know the humor, curiosity, mischief and tenderness behind those words — and to be loved by a man who made my own world more joyful, more adventurous and infinitely more beautiful.”

Donations in his memory can be made to the New York City chapter of the Alzheimer’s Association or to the ASPCA.

#5894. Chase Ink Business Unlimited Review: 75K Bonus, 1.5x Points, & No Annual Fee

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I’d like to take an in-depth look at the Ink Business Unlimited® Credit Card, which is potentially the most compelling Chase business credit card for everyday spending, and part of the Chase Ink Business card portfolio.

The card has no annual fee, an excellent welcome bonus, and a great rewards structure. In conjunction with other cards earning Ultimate Rewards points, this can be one of the most useful cards for a small business.

Link: Learn more about the Ink Business Unlimited® Credit Card with 75K bonus points

Before I get into the full Chase Ink Business Unlimited Card review, here’s a look at the card’s standout features:

Below are what I consider to be the pros and cons of the Chase Ink Business Unlimited Card:

Let’s take a look at the Chase Ink Business Unlimited card welcome bonus, eligibility rules, and annual fee. After all, these are major factors that people consider when deciding which card to apply for. For a no annual fee card in particular, the Chase Ink Unlimited bonus is hard to beat.

The Ink Business Unlimited currently has a welcome bonus of 75,000 points after spending $6,000 within three months of account opening. This is among the best welcome offers you’ll find on any no annual fee card.

On the surface this is a cash back card, meaning that the 75,000 points are worth $750 cash back. However, you can potentially convert these rewards into “full” Ultimate Rewards points. Based on my latest miles & points valuations, Ultimate Rewards points are worth 1.7 cents each, making the bonus worth up to $1,275.

Every credit card comes with its own eligibility terms, though fortunately the Chase Ink Business Unlimited Card has among the less restrictive requirements. As you’ll see when you look at the offer terms for the card, here are the rules:

The new cardmember bonus may not be available to you if you have ever had this card or any other Chase for Business card without an annual fee. We may also consider factors pertinent to your business in determining your bonus eligibility.

If you want to be approved for a Chase Ink card, there are some general restrictions to be aware of:

The Ink Business Unlimited has no annual fee. Getting a no annual fee card that’s super rewarding is rare, especially when you consider the value of the welcome bonus. Not only does the card not have an annual fee for the primary cardmember, but you can also add authorized users at no extra cost.

This is a card that you can keep long term, and it won’t cost you anything.

While the Chase Ink Unlimited is a fantastic card for purchases within the United States, the card does have 3% foreign transaction fees, so I don’t recommend using this card abroad. If you’re looking for a similar card with no foreign transaction fees, consider the Ink Business Preferred® Credit Card.

A welcome bonus is the quickest way to earn points, but a card’s ongoing earning structure matters too. So let’s talk about how you earn rewards with the Ink Business Unlimited.

The Ink Business Unlimited offers 1.5x points on all business purchases, with no limits. This is a straightforward rewards structure, so there’s no need to focus on bonus categories. If you use this card correctly, it’s the single best Chase business card for earning Ultimate Rewards points on everyday spending.

The Chase Ink Business Unlimited is marketed as offering unlimited 1.5% cash back. The catch is that this isn’t actually that good, since there are other cards that earn 2% cash back. But there’s a trick that allows you to convert the 1.5% cash back into 1.5x Ultimate Rewards points per dollar spent. I value Ultimate Rewards points at 1.7 cents each, so suddenly, you’re going from a return of 1.5% to a return of 2.55%, by my valuation.

If you’re earning 1.5x Ultimate Rewards points per dollar spent, I consider this to be the best Chase business card for everyday spending.

The Ink Business Unlimited also earns 5% cash, or 5x points, on Lyft rides. This bonus category is valid through September 30, 2027, and it’s worth taking advantage of if you ever use the rideshare service. That said, the card’s 1.5x points on non-bonus spending is the main attraction.

On the surface, the Ink Business Unlimited is a cash back card, as the name suggests. When I talk about “points” above, each point gets you 1.0 cents cash back. So when the card offers 1.5x points on all business purchases, that means it ordinarily earns the equivalent of 1.5% cash back.

You can redeem cash back from the Ink Business Unlimited as a statement credit or direct deposit to most checking and savings accounts, with no minimum. You can also redeem Ink Business Unlimited rewards through Chase Travel at a flat rate of 1.0 cents per point, or for gift cards.

This is where there’s a trick that can hugely increase the value of your Chase Ink Unlimited points. If you have the Chase Ink Business Unlimited in conjunction with another card that earns “premium” Ultimate Rewards points, then you can do significantly better than 1.0 cent per point in value for redemptions. These cards include the following:

If you have the Ink Unlimited in conjunction with one of those cards, suddenly your points will get you the following when redeeming through Chase Travel℠:

That potentially increases the value of your points significantly, and you can often get even more value out of your points by transferring them to one of the Ultimate Rewards airline or hotel partners.

I value Ultimate Rewards points at 1.7 cents each, so to me that increases the value of the points earned on this card by 70%. Transferring points between cards is easy and can be done online. You can learn more about that process in this post.

For a no annual fee card, the Chase Ink Business Unlimited offers a great welcome bonus and generous rewards structure, though there are also some perks that are potentially quite valuable.

The Ink Business Unlimited offers primary collision damage waiver coverage for rentals of most types of vehicles in most countries, except where it is prohibited by law, by individual merchants, or is in violation of the territory terms of the rental agreement. There aren’t many no annual fee cards offering rental car coverage, so I think that’s pretty remarkable.

Prior to renting, you should check with the benefits administrator (call the number on the back of your card) to verify your coverage. Keep in mind, coverage is only primary when renting for business purposes. For personal rentals within the US, coverage is secondary to your own auto insurance. For primary coverage on personal rentals, look to the Chase Sapphire Preferred® Card (learn more) or Chase Sapphire Reserve® Card (learn more).

In addition to rental car coverage, the Ink Business Unlimited offers other purchase and travel benefits, including (but not limited to) the following:

You’ll want to read the cardmember agreement for exact terms, but this has the potential to be extremely valuable for purchases as well. With the Chase Ink Business Unlimited, you can also extend the time period of your United States manufacturer’s warranty by an additional year, on eligible warranties of three years or less.

I applied for the Chase Ink Business Unlimited Card a couple of years back, for a sole proprietorship, and I’d like to share my experience with that.

Chase’s business card applications are pretty straightforward. The first section just requires completing personal information, which is pretty standard for any credit card application.

The next section asked for business information. You should always answer credit card applications truthfully. If you decide to apply for the card as a sole proprietorship, here are some tips:

I submitted my application, and found that I was instantly approved. I can’t help but think that many Chase business cards are easier to get instant approvals for nowadays. Shortly before that I had applied for the Ink Business Preferred, and received an instant approval, which I’ve previously almost never gotten on a Chase business card.

If you have a small business, the Chase Ink Business Unlimited is absolutely worth applying for, given the bonus, and it’s worth keeping for everyday spending, assuming you use it in conjunction with Ultimate Rewards.

I’d like to look a bit more closely at the best overall strategy to take with the Chase Ink Business Unlimited — what are the best complements, what are the best substitutes, etc.

In many ways the best complements to the Ink Business Unlimited are two of Chase’s premium business cards in the Ultimate Rewards ecosystem, so you can maximize rewards, thanks to the various bonus categories:

If you value Chase points, the Ink Business Cash® Credit Card (learn more) is another Chase card that should be on your list. Like the Ink Business Unlimited, it has no annual fee, and it generally offers a similar welcome bonus. Here’s where it differs:

The better card for you really comes down to which rewards structure you prefer. If you spend a lot at office supply stores, the Chase Ink Business Cash could be a better choice. Just keep in mind that you probably won’t be able to get the welcome bonus from both cards — which shouldn’t be too much of an issue, since they’re often the exact same offer.

Perhaps the best alternative to the Chase Ink Unlimited is The Blue Business® Plus Credit Card from American Express (learn more), which has no annual fee (Rates & Fees), and offers 2x Membership Rewards points on the first $50,000 spent annually (after that you earn 1x points).

I’d say that’s an unbeatable card for everyday, non-bonused business spending, though the catch is that you’re capped at earning 2x points on the first $50,000 spent every calendar year. That’s very restrictive for many companies. Personally I have both cards, and have gotten good value from both of them.

If you’re looking to earn cash back for your business spending, it’s tough to go wrong with The American Express Blue Business Cash® Card (learn more), which has no annual fee ((Rates & Fees)). The card offers 2% cash back on all eligible purchases on the first $50,000 of purchases each calendar year, 1% thereafter, which is a solid cash back return.

The Ink Business Unlimited is a phenomenal no annual fee business credit card, and on top of that, it’s offering a great welcome bonus. Getting $750 cash back (or 75,000 points) as a bonus on a no annual fee card is impressive.

The primary reason to keep this card in the long run is that if you’re looking to earn Ultimate Rewards points, this is the best business card for everyday spending. The card offers 1.5x points on all business purchases, and in conjunction with some other Chase cards, those rewards can be converted to Ultimate Rewards airline and hotel partners. Add in the fact that the card offers great benefits, and this is a no annual fee card you’ll want to keep long term.

#486. ‘Nobody Wants This’ Star Condemns Adam Brody’s Gaza “Genocide” Comments

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In an interview about the new season of the Netflix series, Brody's onscreen mother, Tovah Feldshuh, said, "To accuse the Jewish people of genocide is wild. It’s right out of Hitler’s playbook."

In Nobody Wants This, Tovah Feldshuh plays Bina, the strong-willed Jewish mother of “hot rabbi” Noah, played by Adam Brody.

Before the now-streaming third season of the hit Netflix rom-com series released, Brody made headlines when, in an interview with GQ, the actor said of the war in Gaza, “put me down as thinking it’s a genocide. Put me down as wanting a free Palestine.” He added, “It’s awful that it’s taken so much death and misery, but it does feel like it’s reaching a breaking point that it should have a while ago.” Related Stories Movies Ray Nicholson Levels Up With 'The Mosquito Bowl': "I'm Always Battling for Legitimacy" TV 'The One About Matthew Perry' Review: Netflix's Intimate and Sincere but Overly Padded Docuseries

Brody — whose other on-screen Jewish characters have included Seth Cohen from The O.C. and Seth Morris in Fleishman Is in Trouble — has previously said he “barely got bar mitzvahed and retained nothing from it.” (Jews are both a religious as well as an ethnic group.) In Nobody Wants This, Brody’s Noah falls in love with the non-Jewish Joanne (Kristen Bell). They then navigate the obstacles — religious and otherwise — of their relationship, leading to a big update for the couple at the end of season three.

When asked about Brody’s comments, Feldshuh, who is a Jewish veteran Broadway, television and film actress, told The New York Post that “to accuse the Jewish people of genocide is wild. It’s right out of Hitler’s playbook. You take the victim and you make the victim the perpetrator.”

Feldshuh added that comments like Brody’s are made worse when coming from Jewish people with large platforms. “[It] makes a difference to the [Jewish] community,” she said. “I believe that the perseverance of the existence of the state of Israel is crucial to the well-being of the democratic world and of the free world. Of course, Israel needs the United States, [but] the United States needs Israel, believe you me.”

Still, she told the outlet there weren’t political disagreements on set.

“My job, as Bina but also as Tovah, is to love the people on set at my place of work, to be kind,” she said. “With the backdrop of global antisemitism. I am thrilled to be chosen to play a Jewish matriarch. Thrilled and proud, and that will never change.”

Nobody Wants This creator Erin Foster has also weighed in on Brody’s comments while promoting season three, saying on the Not Skinny But Not Fat podcast that “it’s complicated.”

“Our work environment, our set is very similar to dinner tables around the world where people are having debate and discussion and disagreeing,” she said. “While I love Adam, I also disagree with him, and that’s okay.”

She added that, as the show’s creator, “all I can say is that I want our Jewish fans to know that I see you and I hear you and I understand how you feel. And the show has always been and always will be a love letter to Judaism. That is why I made this show. It’s because of how beautiful of an experience I’ve had becoming Jewish and being a Jewish person, and sitting here right now at this time in the world where there is almost zero positive representation of Jewish people, I think it’s so important to have that.

“That being said, everyone is entitled to a different opinion than me. I can just tell you what I created this show for, what the intention of the show is,” Foster concluded.

Rabbi Steve Leder — the former senior rabbi of Wilshire Boulevard Temple in Los Angeles (the synagogue where Foster converted to Judaism) and who was the technical adviser during season one of the show — also condemned Brody’s comments, saying, “You by your own admission, are a relatively ignorant Jew, and your ignorance really showed the other day. Here’s some technical advice: If you know nothing about something, you should say nothing about it.”

In season one, Leder consulted with the cast and crew to ensure the Jewish representation, including Brody’s portrayal of a rabbi, felt authentic. “There is no genocide in Gaza, there are genocides in other countries happening, but you failed to mention any of them,” added Leder in his response. “If you’re going to use a word like genocide, you better know what it means. And by the way, being so flippant about a word like genocide — ‘hey, put me down for genocide’ like you’re asking for a table for two at [L.A. hotspot] Craig’s — is disgusting.”

#5350. Volkswagen’s EV recall spreads to Audi, and owners are told to stop at 80% charge

📅 Oct 27, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Volkswagen’s latest recall now extends to Audi, affecting more than 9,000 Q4 E-Tron and Q4 Sportback E-Tron vehicles. And just like with the ID.4, owners are being told to limit charging to 80% of the battery’s capacity.

Earlier this month, Volkswagen issued a recall impacting 22,524 ID.4 electric SUVs, model years 2023 to 2026.

The recall is due to the risk of thermal propagation, or a battery fire, under certain conditions when charged to over 80% of its limit.

Volkswagen said in a safety recall report with the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) that “certain charging profiles combined with a state of charge over 80% may trigger thermal propagation.”

While the root cause is unknown, Volkswagen urged owners to limit charging to 80%. Owners of affected vehicles received an email saying VW “intends to apply a temporary vehicle setting that limits charging to 80%,” unless they opted out by October 21.

In the letter sent to the NHTSA on October 23, Volkswagen Group of America said it was issuing a recall affecting 9,048 Audi Q4 E-Tron and Q4 Sportback E-Tron vehicles model years 2024 to 2025.

The safety recall follows Audi’s Product Safety Committee being informed of battery thermal events involving other Volkswagen Group EVs that use the same batteries as the Audi Q4 E-Tron.

Following an internal investigation, Audi found the same battery and production characteristics as the impacted VW Group vehicles. Audi said the majority of these were produced for the North American market.

Audi is not aware of any claims, crashes, fires, injuries, or deaths associated with the Q4 E-Tron’s battery issue.

The root cause has yet to be determined, and until a remedy is available, Audi is advising owners to limit charging to 80%.

Owners can take their vehicles to an authorized Audi dealer, where the dealer will run a diagnostic check on the battery. If needed, the dealer will impose a temporary state-of-charge limit to 80%.

Audi expects to mail out interim letters notifying owners of the safety risks on December 18, 2026. Additional letters will send additional letters once a remedy is available.

Owners can contact Audi’s customer service at 1-800-253-2834 with questions or concerns. Audi’s recall number is 93SK. You can also contact the NHTSA Hotline at 1-888-327-4236 or visit nhtsa.gov for more information. The NHTSA campaign number is 26V698.

Volkswagen’s battery recall doesn’t just affect electric vehicles in the US. Last week, Volkswagen Group issued three separate recalls with the German Federal Motor Transport Authority (KBA), affecting 72,668 EVs worldwide, including 9,881 in Germany.

The recall affects certain batches of the ID. Buzz, ID.3, ID.4, and ID.7 models produced between 2022 and 2025, as well as Cupra Born models from 2022 to 2024.
